            db.execute("INSERT INTO outbox (topic, payload) VALUES (?,?)",
                       ("expense.created",
                        json.dumps({"id": expense_id, "tenant": who.tenant})))
            out = to_out(load(who.tenant, expense_id))
            if idempotency_key:
                db.execute("UPDATE idempotency SET state='completed', status=?, body=? "
                           "WHERE tenant_id=? AND key=?",
                           (201, out.model_dump_json(), who.tenant, idempotency_key))
        response.headers["Location"] = f"/expenses/{expense_id}"   # 201 always says where
        response.headers["ETag"] = etag_of(load(who.tenant, expense_id))
        return out

    @app.get("/expenses/{expense_id}", response_model=None)
    def read(expense_id: str, response: Response,
             who: Annotated[Principal, Depends(require("expenses:read"))],
             if_none_match: Annotated[str | None, Header()] = None):
        row = load(who.tenant, expense_id)
        tag = etag_of(row)
        if if_none_match == tag:
            return Response(status_code=304,                       # no body, ever (§10.5)
                            headers={"ETag": tag,
                                     "Cache-Control": "private, max-age=30"})
        response.headers["ETag"] = tag
        response.headers["Cache-Control"] = "private, max-age=30"  # private: personal data
        return to_out(row)

    @app.patch("/expenses/{expense_id}", response_model=ExpenseOut)
    def update(expense_id: str, body: ExpensePatch, response: Response,
               who: Annotated[Principal, Depends(require("expenses:write"))],
               if_match: Annotated[str | None, Header()] = None) -> ExpenseOut:
        row = load(who.tenant, expense_id)
        if if_match is None:
            raise PreconditionRequired("If-Match")     # 428: the guard is not optional
        if if_match != etag_of(row):
            raise PreconditionFailed("the expense changed since you read it")
        changes = body.model_dump(exclude_unset=True)  # absent != null (§4.2)
        if row["status"] == "approved" and "status" in changes:
            raise Conflict("an approved expense cannot change status")
        with db:
            for field, column in (("description", "description"), ("status", "status")):
                if field in changes:
                    db.execute(f"UPDATE expenses SET {column}=? "
                               "WHERE tenant_id=? AND id=?",
                               (changes[field], who.tenant, expense_id))
            db.execute("UPDATE expenses SET version=version+1 "
                       "WHERE tenant_id=? AND id=?", (who.tenant, expense_id))
        updated = load(who.tenant, expense_id)
        response.headers["ETag"] = etag_of(updated)    # the new validator, so no re-read
        return to_out(updated)

    @app.delete("/expenses/{expense_id}", status_code=204)
    def soft_delete(expense_id: str,
                    who: Annotated[Principal,
                                   Depends(require("expenses:write"))]) -> Response:
        load(who.tenant, expense_id)
        with db:
            db.execute("UPDATE expenses SET deleted_at=? WHERE tenant_id=? AND id=?",
                       ("2026-03-04T10:02:00Z", who.tenant, expense_id))
        return Response(status_code=204)

    @app.get("/expenses", response_model=Page)
    def list_expenses(who: Annotated[Principal, Depends(require("expenses:read"))],
                      limit: Annotated[int, Query(ge=1, le=100)] = 20,
                      pageToken: str | None = None,
                      status: str | None = None) -> Page:
        sql = "SELECT * FROM expenses WHERE tenant_id=? AND deleted_at IS NULL"
        args: list[Any] = [who.tenant]                 # the tenant, from the token
        if status is not None:
            sql += " AND status=?"                     # an allowlisted filter (§7.5)
            args.append(status)
        if pageToken:
            cursor = json.loads(unb64(pageToken))
            sql += " AND (created_at, id) < (?, ?)"    # the keyset seek (§7.2)
            args += [cursor["created_at"], cursor["id"]]
        sql += " ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT ?"   # total order (§7.3)
        args.append(limit + 1)                         # +1 answers "is there a next page"
        rows = db.execute(sql, args).fetchall()
        page, more = rows[:limit], len(rows) > limit
        token = b64(json.dumps({"created_at": page[-1]["created_at"],
                                "id": page[-1]["id"]}).encode()) if more else None
        return Page(items=[to_out(r) for r in page], nextPageToken=token)

    @app.get("/healthz", include_in_schema=False)
    def healthz() -> dict:
        return {"status": "ok"}                        # liveness: no I/O at all (§15.4)

    return app

16.5 The Relay

The outbox is worthless without something to drain it. In production this is a separate process taking a lock; here it is nine lines, and the important property is the same — publishing happens after the commit, so a crash before publishing leaves a row to retry rather than an event that never existed.

def relay(db: sqlite3.Connection, publish) -> int:
    rows = db.execute("SELECT id, topic, payload FROM outbox "
                      "WHERE published_at IS NULL ORDER BY id").fetchall()
    for row in rows:
        publish(row["topic"], json.loads(row["payload"]))
        db.execute("UPDATE outbox SET published_at='now' WHERE id=?", (row["id"],))
    db.commit()
    return len(rows)

16.6 Where Each Section Shows Up

Section In ledger
§2 methods and codes 201+Location, 204, 304, 401+WWW-Authenticate, 403, 404, 412, 422, 428, 429+Retry-After
§3 resource modelling /expenses and /expenses/{id}; nothing is a verb
§4 bodies integer minor units, RFC 3339 timestamps, string IDs, exclude_unset on PATCH
§5 errors one taxonomy, one mapping table, application/problem+json, a requestId on every problem
§6 validation extra="forbid", bounded fields, separate in/patch/out models
§7 pagination keyset cursor, limit capped at 100, total ordering, soft delete excluded
§8 authentication bearer token, 401 with a challenge, authentication applied globally
§9 authorization scopes, tenant from the credential, 404 for another tenant's row
§10 caching weak ETag, If-None-Match → 304, If-Match → 412, private cache directive
§11 idempotency Idempotency-Key with fingerprint, replay and conflict; token-bucket rate limit
§12 transactions one transaction covering the row, the event and the idempotency record
§14 contracts a generated OpenAPI 3.1 document, one operation per endpoint
§15 observability requestId echoed into problem documents; /healthz with no I/O

16.7 Driving It

The whole service runs in-process through httpx.ASGITransport — no ports, no server, and fast enough to be a test suite.

import asyncio
import httpx

ADA = {"Authorization": "Bearer tok_ada"}
GRACE = {"Authorization": "Bearer tok_grace"}
RO = {"Authorization": "Bearer tok_ro"}

async def main() -> None:
    db, clock = connect(), [0.0]
    app = build_app(db, clock)
    transport = httpx.ASGITransport(app=app)
    async with httpx.AsyncClient(transport=transport, base_url="http://ledger") as c:

        clock[0] += 60.0
        print("== creation is idempotent ==")
        body = {"description": "Taxi to airport", "amountMinor": 4250, "currency": "GBP"}
        r1 = await c.post("/expenses", json=body, headers=ADA | {"Idempotency-Key": "k1"})
        r2 = await c.post("/expenses", json=body, headers=ADA | {"Idempotency-Key": "k1"})
        eid = r1.json()["id"]
        print(f"first  {r1.status_code} Location={r1.headers['location']}")
        print(f"retry  {r2.status_code} same id={r2.json()['id'] == eid}")
        r = await c.post("/expenses", json={**body, "amountMinor": 9999},
                         headers=ADA | {"Idempotency-Key": "k1"})
        print(f"reused key, new body -> {r.status_code}")

        clock[0] += 60.0
        print("== validation at the boundary ==")
        r = await c.post("/expenses", json={**body, "amountMinor": -5}, headers=ADA)
        print("negative amount ->", r.status_code, r.json()["detail"][0]["type"])
        r = await c.post("/expenses", json={**body, "isApproved": True}, headers=ADA)
        print("unknown field   ->", r.status_code, r.json()["detail"][0]["type"])

        clock[0] += 60.0
        print("== caching and optimistic concurrency ==")
        r = await c.get(f"/expenses/{eid}", headers=ADA)
        tag = r.headers["etag"]
        print("read     ", r.status_code, tag, r.headers["cache-control"])
        r = await c.get(f"/expenses/{eid}", headers=ADA | {"If-None-Match": tag})
        print("revalidate", r.status_code, "bytes", len(r.content))
        r = await c.patch(f"/expenses/{eid}", json={"status": "submitted"},
                          headers=ADA | {"If-Match": tag})
        print("alice     ", r.status_code, r.headers["etag"])
        r = await c.patch(f"/expenses/{eid}", json={"description": "Taxi"},
                          headers=ADA | {"If-Match": tag})
        print("bob (stale)", r.status_code, r.json()["type"].rsplit("/", 1)[1])
        r = await c.patch(f"/expenses/{eid}", json={"description": "Taxi"}, headers=ADA)
        print("no If-Match", r.status_code, r.json()["type"].rsplit("/", 1)[1])

        clock[0] += 60.0
        print("== authorization ==")
        r = await c.get(f"/expenses/{eid}", headers=GRACE)
        print("other tenant ->", r.status_code, "(404, not 403)")
        r = await c.patch(f"/expenses/{eid}", json={"status": "approved"},
                          headers=RO | {"If-Match": "x"})
        print("read-only scope ->", r.status_code)
        r = await c.get("/expenses", headers={"Authorization": "Bearer nope"})
        print("bad token ->", r.status_code, r.headers.get("www-authenticate"))

        clock[0] += 60.0
        print("== pagination ==")
        for n in range(6):
            clock[0] += 2.0
            await c.post("/expenses", json={"description": f"Lunch {n}",
                                            "amountMinor": 100 + n, "currency": "GBP"},
                         headers=ADA)
        clock[0] += 60.0
        seen, token, pages = [], None, 0
        while True:
            params = {"limit": 3} | ({"pageToken": token} if token else {})
            r = await c.get("/expenses", params=params, headers=ADA)
            page = r.json()
            pages += 1
            seen += [i["id"] for i in page["items"]]
            token = page["nextPageToken"]
            if token is None:
                break
        print(f"walked {pages} pages, {len(seen)} items,"
              f" unique={len(set(seen)) == len(seen)}")

        clock[0] += 60.0
        print("== soft delete ==")
        r = await c.delete(f"/expenses/{eid}", headers=ADA)
        after = await c.get(f"/expenses/{eid}", headers=ADA)
        listed = await c.get("/expenses", params={"limit": 100}, headers=ADA)
        print("delete", r.status_code, "| then GET", after.status_code,
              "| still listed:", eid in [i["id"] for i in listed.json()["items"]])

        print("== rate limiting ==")
        codes = []
        for _ in range(8):
            codes.append((await c.get("/healthz", headers=RO)).status_code)
        last = await c.get("/healthz", headers=RO)
        print("codes:", codes, "| Retry-After:", last.headers.get("retry-after"))

        print("== the outbox ==")
        published: list = []
        print("events relayed:", relay(db, lambda t, p: published.append(t)),
              "| topics:", sorted(set(published)))

        print("== the contract ==")
        spec = app.openapi()
        print("openapi", spec["openapi"], "| operations:",
              sum(len(v) for v in spec["paths"].values()))

asyncio.run(main())

16.8 The Transcript

== creation is idempotent ==
first  201 Location=/expenses/exp_f19d3e15
retry  201 same id=True
reused key, new body -> 422
== validation at the boundary ==
negative amount -> 422 greater_than
unknown field   -> 422 extra_forbidden
== caching and optimistic concurrency ==
read      200 W/"1-exp_f19d3e15" private, max-age=30
revalidate 304 bytes 0
alice      200 W/"2-exp_f19d3e15"
bob (stale) 412 precondition-failed
no If-Match 428 precondition-required
== authorization ==
other tenant -> 404 (404, not 403)
read-only scope -> 403
bad token -> 401 Bearer realm="ledger"
== pagination ==
walked 3 pages, 7 items, unique=True
== soft delete ==
delete 204 | then GET 404 | still listed: False
== rate limiting ==
codes: [200, 200, 200, 200, 200, 429, 429, 429] | Retry-After: 1
== the outbox ==
events relayed: 7 | topics: ['expense.created']
== the contract ==
openapi 3.1.0 | operations: 5

Every line is worth reading once more, because each is a section of this course being true rather than being asserted.

retry 201 same id=True — the second POST did no work and returned the first one's 201, body and status code intact. reused key, new body -> 422 — the fingerprint caught a key being reused for a different request, which is the check that stops one client's mistake from returning another's response.

revalidate 304 bytes 0 — the second read transferred no body at all. bob (stale) 412 next to alice 200 is a lost update that did not happen: Alice's write moved the version, so Bob's stale validator was refused rather than silently overwriting her. no If-Match 428 is the same protection made non-optional.

other tenant -> 404 is the §9.2 decision in one line: Grace's request for Ada's expense is answered as though it does not exist, because confirming it exists is itself the leak. read-only scope -> 403 is authorization refusing what authentication already permitted.

walked 3 pages, 7 items, unique=True — every expense appeared exactly once. Note that all seven share a created_at, so the walk is being held together entirely by the id tie-breaker (§7.3); without it, this line would fail intermittently. still listed: False after a 204 is soft delete behaving correctly on both paths.

codes: [200 × 5, 429 × 3] is the token bucket absorbing a burst of five and then refusing, with a concrete Retry-After rather than a bare refusal. events relayed: 7 matches the seven expenses exactly — no event without a row, and no row without an event, because they commit together.

16.9 What Is Deliberately Missing

ledger is 300 lines, and honesty about its gaps is more useful than pretending it is production-ready. Every omission below is a section of this course that a real service would need:

  • Real authentication. TOKENS is a dictionary. Production verifies a JWT's signature, issuer, audience and expiry (§8.6), or looks up a session.
  • A durable database. SQLite in memory has no connection pool, no concurrent writers, and no row-level security — which §9.5 argues should be the tenant backstop.
  • A relay that can run twice. relay takes no lock, so two instances would publish every event twice (§12.3).
  • Observability. There is no structured log line, no trace context and no metrics (§15.1–§15.3). The requestId is echoed but never recorded, which is half a mechanism.
  • Versioning. No Api-Version, no deprecation machinery, no path prefix (§14.5).
  • Graceful shutdown and readiness. /healthz exists; /readyz does not (§15.4).
  • An asynchronous path. Nothing here takes longer than a few milliseconds, so there is no 202, no status resource and no queue (§12.4, §12.5).
  • Tests. The transcript is a demonstration, not a suite. A real one asserts each line rather than printing it, and adds the cross-account authorization test from §9.1.

Where to Go Next

Three things are worth doing with this material, in order.

Take an API you already own and audit it against §16.6's table. Most services score well on the first three rows and poorly on caching, idempotency and error taxonomy — and those three are where the operational pain lives. Fixing an error taxonomy is a week and changes every support conversation afterwards.

Read one specification properly. RFC 9110 is long but not difficult, and §9 (methods) and §15 (status codes) can be read in an afternoon. Almost everyone building HTTP APIs is working from folklore about what these mean; being the person who has read the document is a durable advantage.

Write down your API's contract before your next endpoint. Not the code — the request, the response, the errors, the headers. It takes twenty minutes and it is the single habit that most reliably converts the material in this course from something you have read into something you do.

You started this course able to build an endpoint. You can now design an interface that other people will still be able to use, extend and operate in three years — which is a different skill, and the one that separates a service from an API.

What you can now do

  1. Read HTTP as a contract rather than a transport. Methods have three defined properties, status codes make claims about fault, headers carry the parts of the agreement that machines act on, and most "clever" API designs are re-implementations of something already in RFC 9110.
  2. Model resources rather than transcribing a schema. Find the nouns, name them consistently, nest only where the parent is part of the child's identity, and turn a stubborn verb into a resource — which buys idempotency for free.
  3. Design payloads that survive contact with other languages. Integer minor units, RFC 3339 timestamps, string identifiers, one case convention, and a deliberate answer to the difference between absent, null and empty.
  4. Return errors people can act on. A problem type that is stable, a message that is not, structured detail for the machine, a correlation identifier for the human, and nothing that helps an attacker.
  5. Draw the trust boundary once. Parse into types that cannot hold invalid data, allowlist what may be set and what may be seen, bound every size, and treat an upstream response as input.
  6. Page through a moving collection without losing records. Keyset cursors, a total ordering, an opaque token, and the knowledge of exactly why offset pagination is not an option.
  7. Choose an authentication mechanism on purpose. Sessions or tokens, authorization code with PKCE, what a JWT does and does not prove, and refresh rotation with reuse detection.
  8. Authorize every object, on every path. BOLA and BFLA are the top of the OWASP API list because they are easy to miss and trivial to exploit; you now know where the check belongs and how to test that it is there.
  9. Use HTTP's caching and concurrency machinery. Cache-Control, Vary, validators, conditional requests, and If-Match — one mechanism that gives you both a cache that does not leak and writes that cannot silently overwrite each other.
  10. Make retries safe and failure survivable. Idempotency keys, jittered exponential backoff, timeout budgets, circuit breakers, rate limits and bounded queues — the difference between a dependency's outage and your own.
  11. Get the transaction boundary right. One write inside the transaction, everything else derived from it, an outbox instead of a dual write, and consumers that are idempotent because delivery is at-least-once.
  12. Evolve without breaking anyone. A written contract, a clear definition of a breaking change, a versioning strategy you can live with, and a deprecation process that ends in 410 Gone on the announced date.
  13. Operate what you built. Structured logs with route templates, a correlation ID that reaches the customer, trace context that survives a queue, and liveness and readiness probes that answer different questions.

That is the working set. There is no part of a normal HTTP API you should now find arbitrary.

When an API decision is genuinely hard

Most of them are not, and the ones that are usually reduce to one of four tensions. It is worth naming them, because recognising which one you are in is most of the work.

Simplicity against capability. A filter grammar, a field mask, a GraphQL layer — each solves a real problem and each adds a surface you own forever. The question to ask is not "is this useful" but "which specific client need is this, and what is the smallest thing that meets it".

Consistency against correctness. You will find an endpoint that returns 409 where the rest of the API returns 422. Changing it is a breaking change; leaving it is a wart every integrator trips over. Usually: leave it, document it, and fix it at the next version boundary — being predictably imperfect costs less than being unpredictably right.

The client's convenience against the contract's stability. Inlining a related object saves a round trip and welds two resources together. Returning the created resource saves a GET and commits you to a response shape. Both are usually worth it; both should be decisions.

What you can guarantee against what you would like to. Exactly-once delivery, a consistent snapshot across a paged walk, an atomic operation spanning two services. In each case the honest move is to state the weaker guarantee plainly in your documentation and give clients the tool to compensate — an idempotency key, a change feed, a status resource — rather than implying a promise the system cannot keep.

Where the material goes next

Four directions, each of which now has a foundation under it:

  • Networking and HTTP below this layer — connection reuse, HTTP/2 multiplexing and HTTP/3, TLS handshakes, and where latency actually comes from.
  • Distributed systems — consensus, replication, partitioning and the failure modes that make §12 necessary in the first place.
  • Data modelling at scale — the SQL course's later sections, plus the indexing and query planning that turn a correct list endpoint into a fast one.
  • Operations — the DevOps course, for the deployment, rollout and incident practice that §15 assumes exists.

The thread running through all four is the same one running through this course: a system is defined by what it promises when things go wrong. Anything works when nothing fails. Design for the other case, say plainly what you guarantee, and give your clients the tools to handle the rest — and you will have built something people can rely on.
